(t,\gamma,\mu,\eta)\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{M}_{2}^{C},

X
and
X^{\prime}
are diffusion processes given by (
33
) and (
34
) respectively.

(i)

Suppose that
f\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1}_{s}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d})
(
\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d})
, resp.). For any
(\tau,\tilde{\omega})\in[0,t]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d},
derivatives

\partial_{t}f(t,\omega,\mu),\ (\partial_{\omega_{\tau}},\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}^{2})f(t,\omega_{\tau},\mu),\ \partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,\mu_{\tau},\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}),\ \text{and }\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}}\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,\mu_{\tau},\tilde{\omega}_{\tau})),

(
\partial_{t}f,\ (\partial_{\omega},\partial_{\omega}^{2})f,\ \partial_{\mu}f,\ \text{and }\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}f)
, resp.)
defined as in (
80
) do not depend on the choice of the extended functional.

(ii)

Suppose that
f\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1}_{p}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}).
For any
s\geq t,
we have

(81)

\begin{split}&f(s,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}})-f(t,\gamma,\mathcal{L}_{\eta})\\
&\ \ =\int_{t}^{s}\partial_{r}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\partial_{\omega}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}})dX(r)\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}})d\langle X\rangle(r)]+\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}[\int_{t}^{s}\partial_{\mu}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}},\tilde{X}^{\prime})d\tilde{X}^{\prime}(r)]\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}\int_{t}^{s}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}},\tilde{X}^{\prime})\tilde{d}(r)\tilde{d}(r)^{T}]dr.\end{split}

(iii)

Suppose that
f\in\mathscr{C}^{0,2,1,1}_{s,p}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}).
For any
t\leq s\leq s^{\prime},
we have the partial Itô-Dupire formula

(82)

\begin{split}&f(s^{\prime},X_{s},\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}_{s}})-f(s^{\prime},\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})\\
&\ \ =\int_{t}^{s}\partial_{\omega_{r}}f(s^{\prime},X_{r},\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}_{r}})dX(r)\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{s}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega_{r}}^{2}f(s^{\prime},X_{r},\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}_{r}})d\langle X\rangle(r)]+\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}[\int_{t}^{s}\partial_{\mu_{r}}f(s^{\prime},X_{r},\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}_{r}},\tilde{X}^{\prime}_{r})d\tilde{X}^{\prime}(r)]\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{P}^{\prime}}\int_{t}^{s}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{r}}\partial_{\mu_{r}}f(s^{\prime},X_{r},\mathcal{L}_{X^{\prime}_{r}},\tilde{X}^{\prime}_{r})\tilde{d}(r)\tilde{d}(r)^{T}]dr.\end{split}

Proof.

Since for any
n\times n
matrix
A
and symmetric
n\times n
matrix
B
,
\text{Tr}[AB]
depends only on Sym
(A)
,

(ii)
and
(iii)
follow from
(i)
, Theorem
2.16
and Corollary
2.17
directly.
To end the proof, we only need to show
(i)
. Indeed, the uniqueness of
\partial_{t}f(t,\gamma,\mu)
follows from its definition. For the uniqueness of
(\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}},\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{2})f(t,\gamma_{\tau},\mu)
, without loss of generality, assume
\tau=t.
Otherwise consider the non-anticipative path-dependent function
\tilde{f}_{\mu_{t}}(\tau,\gamma):=f(t,\gamma_{\tau},\mu)
instead of
f(t,\gamma_{\tau},\mu).
For any
(\gamma,\mu)\in\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C},
take
c(\cdot)=d(\cdot)=0
in equation (
34
) for
X^{\prime}
and
a=0
in equation (
33
) for X. For any extension
F
of
f,
applying Itô formula (
35
) to
F(s,X,\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})
on
s\in[t,T]
, we have

(83)

\begin{split}f(T,X,\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})-f(t,\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})=&\ \int_{t}^{T}\partial_{r}f(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})dr+\int_{t}^{T}\partial_{\omega_{r}}F(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})b(r)dB(r)\\
&\ \ +\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{T}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega_{r}}^{2}F(r,X,\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}})b(r)b(r)^{T}]dr.\end{split}

In view of identity (
83
) and the Doob-Meyer theorem for semimartingales, we obtain the uniqueness of
\partial_{\omega_{t}}F(t,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t})
and
\text{Sym}(\partial_{\omega_{t}}^{2}F(t,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t}))
. For the uniqueness of
\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f
and
\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}}\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f)
, again we assume
\tau=t
. Otherwise consider
\bar{f}_{\omega_{t}}(\tau,\mu)=f(t,\omega,\mu_{\tau})
, and then by definition

\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,\mu_{\tau},\tilde{\omega}_{\tau})=\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}\bar{f}_{\omega_{t}}(\tau,\mu,\tilde{\omega}),\ \ \text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}}\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,\mu_{\tau},\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}))=\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{\tau}}\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}\bar{f}_{\omega_{t}}(\tau,\mu,\tilde{\omega})).

Then the uniqueness of
\partial_{\mu}f
and
\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}f)
follows from
[
55
, Theorem 2.9]
.

∎

Remark 3.6
.

The uniqueness of
\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}f
can be proved via a similar argument as above from the uniqueness of
\partial_{\mu}f
under a stronger assumption on the regularity of
f
. However, our Itô-Dupire formulas and analysis below only depend on
\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}f)
. Indeed, equation (
3.2
) also only depends on
\text{Sym}(\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u)
instead of
\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u.

For the case
(\gamma,\mu)\in\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}
, we assume

(A2)

\Phi:\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
such that there exists
\Phi^{\prime}
satisfying
(H2)
(i) and
\Phi=\Phi^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}
.
f:[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
such that there exists
f^{\prime}
satisfying
(H2)
(ii) and
f=f^{\prime}
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

A functional
u\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1}_{p}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d})
is called a classical solution to equation (
3.2
) if it satisfies equation (
3.2
). The following theorem states the uniqueness of solutions for equation (
3.2
).

Theorem 3.7
.

Suppose that
u^{1}
and
u^{2}
are two classical solutions to the path-dependent master equation (
3.2
). Then
u^{1}=u^{2}
.

Proof.

Apply Itô formula (
81
) to
u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})
on
r\in[t,s],

i=1,2,
and we obtain that

\begin{split}du_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})=&\ \partial_{r}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})dr+\partial_{\omega}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})dB(r)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}[\partial^{2}_{\omega}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})]dr\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\text{Tr}[\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})dr]\right].\end{split}

In view of equation (
3.2
), we have

\begin{split}du_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})=&\ -f(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}}),\partial_{\omega}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}}),\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{u_{i}(r,{B}^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})})dr\\
&\ +\partial_{\omega}u_{i}(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})dB(r).\end{split}

Then processes
(Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}})
and
(Y^{\eta_{t}},Z^{\eta_{t}})
given by

\displaystyle(Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(s),Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(s)):=(u_{i}(s,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}}),\partial_{\omega}u_{i}(s,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle(Y^{\eta_{t}}(s),Z^{\eta_{t}}(s)):=(u_{i}(s,B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}}),\partial_{\omega}u_{i}(s,B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}})),\ \ \ \ s\geq t,

define solutions to equations (
56
) and (
57
), respectively.
By the uniqueness of solutions for BSDEs (
56
) and (
57
), our conclusion follows.

∎

Now we show the existence of a classical solution to (
3.2
) via FBSDEs.

Theorem 3.8
.

Suppose that
(f,\Phi)
satisfies Assumption
(A2)
and
u
is given by (
58
). Then
u
restricted on
\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}
is a classical solution of (
3.2
).

Proof.

In view of Proposition
3.3
, we have
u\in\mathscr{C}_{s,p}^{0,2,1,1,}(\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}).
For any
(t,\gamma,\mu)\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}

and
h>0,

(84)

\begin{split}&u(t+h,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t})-u(t,\gamma,\mu)\\
&\ \ \ =u(t+h,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}})\right]-u(t,\gamma,\mu).\end{split}

Applying partial Itô formula (
82
) to
u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}})
on
r\in[t,t+h]
, we have

(85)

\begin{split}&u(t+h,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t})-u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}})\\
&\ \ \ =-\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial_{\omega_{r}}u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}})dB(r)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega_{r}}^{2}u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}})]dr\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ -\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{t}^{t+h}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{r}}\partial_{\mu_{r}}u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}_{r})]dr.\end{split}

On the other hand, in view of identities (
65
), (
61
), and BSDE (
56
)

(86)

\begin{split}&u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}})-u(t,\gamma,\mu)=Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(t+h)-Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(t)\\
&\quad=-\int_{t}^{t+h}f(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}})dr+\int_{t}^{t+h}Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(r)dB(r).\end{split}

Putting (
85
) and (
86
) to (
84
), and taking expectation
\mathbb{E}
, we obtain

(87)

\begin{split}u(t+h,\gamma_{t},\mu_{t})-u(t,\gamma,\mu)&=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\mathbb{E}\left[\text{Tr}[\partial_{\omega_{r}}^{2}u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}})]\right]dr\\
&\ \ \ -\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{t+h}\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\text{Tr}[\partial_{\tilde{\omega}_{r}}\partial_{\mu_{r}}u(t+h,B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}_{r}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}_{r})]\right]dr\\
&\ \ \ -\int_{t}^{t+h}\mathbb{E}\left[f(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}})\right]dr.\end{split}

Moreover, in view of (
61
), (
65
) and (
66
), we have

(88)

\displaystyle Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(r)=u(r,B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}}),\ Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}({r})=\partial_{\gamma_{t}}u(r,B^{\omega_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}}),

(89)

\displaystyle\quad\text{and }\quad Y^{\eta_{t}}(r)=u(r,B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}}).

Then dividing both sides of (
87
) by
h
and taking
h\rightarrow 0^{+}
, according to the dominated convergence theorem and Proposition
3.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u(t,\gamma,\mu)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ \left[\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma,\mu)\right]-\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\mu}[\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u(t,\gamma,\mu,W)]\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \ -f\left(t,\gamma,u(t,\gamma,\mu),\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma,\mu),\mu,\mathcal{L}_{u(t,B^{\eta_{t}},\mu)}\right).

∎

3.3.
Classical solution of semi-linear path-dependent PDEs

As stated in the introduction, a classical solution to a semi-linear equation (
1
) suffers from several problems if one tries to build the solution via the classical argument of FBSDE theory. In the following, we approximate the classical solution to (
1
) via a sequence of solutions to corresponding “coefficient frozen” equations. For simplicity of technique and notations, here we only consider the measure independent case and assume
f
is independent of
z
-variable, which is also new even restricted in the path-dependent setting,

(90)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\omega)\sigma(\omega_{t})\sigma(\omega_{t})^{T}]+\partial_{\omega}u(t,\omega)b(\omega_{t})+f(t,\omega,u(t,\omega))=0,\\
\\
u(T,\omega,\mu)=\Phi(\omega),\ \ \ (t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

However, see Remark
3.16
for the general and mean-field case.
Instead of considering a forward SDE, we
consider the following “coefficient frozen” SDE

(91)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s):=\gamma(t)+b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})(s-t)+\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})(B_{s}-B_{t}),\quad s\geq t>0,\\[5.69054pt]
X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{t}=\gamma_{t}.\end{array}\right.

Here and in the following
\varepsilon>0
is a small parameter.
According to the above definition, we have that
X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{t},
and for any strongly vertically differentiable functional
\Phi:\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},

(92)

\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}[\Phi(X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}})]=\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}),\ \ \ \forall\tau\in(t-\varepsilon,t].

In view of the FBSDE argument, we consider the following path-dependent BSDE,

(93)

Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)=\Phi(X_{T}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}})+\int_{s}^{T}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))dr-\int_{t}^{T}Z^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r)dB(r),\ \ \ s\geq t.

In view of (
92
), the following result follows from the differentiability of the corresponding BSDEs shown in Proposition
4.6
.

Corollary 3.9
.

Assume that
(f,\Phi)
satisfies assumption
(A2)
and fix
t\in(0,T].

For any
(\tau,\gamma)\in(t-\varepsilon,t]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d},

Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)
is twice strongly vertically differentiable at
(\tau,t,\gamma)
for any
s\geq t.
Moreover, derivatives
\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}
and
\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{2}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}
are solutions to (
94
) and (
95
) respectively.

(94)

\begin{split}\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)=&\ \partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{T})+\int_{s}^{T}\Big[\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\\
&\ +\partial_{y}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\Big]dr-\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Z^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r)dB(r).\end{split}

(95)

\begin{split}\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{2}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)=&\ \partial_{\omega_{\tau}}^{2}\Phi(X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{T})+\int_{s}^{T}\Big[\partial_{y}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{2}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r)\\
&\ +\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\partial_{y}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))+\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}^{2}f(r,X_{r}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))\Big]dr\\
&\ -\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}^{2}Z^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r)dB(r).\end{split}

Let

(96)

u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma):=Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(t),\ \ \ \forall(t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d},

and it follows from the above corollary that
u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)
is twice strongly vertically differentiable at
(\tau,t,\gamma)
for any
\tau\in(t-\varepsilon,t].
Then according to Itô’s formula (
82
), we have that for any
h\in(0,\varepsilon),

(97)

\begin{split}&u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{t+h})-u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})\\
&\ \ \ =\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial_{\omega_{r}}u(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r})b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})dr+\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial_{\omega_{r}}u(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r})\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})dB(r)\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial^{2}_{\omega_{r}}u(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r})\sigma^{2}(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})dr.\end{split}

Here and in the following we assume
d=1
for simplicity.
On the other hand, according to a classical argument as in
[
46
, Lemma 4.4]
(also see Proposition
3.2
), we have

(98)

u^{\varepsilon}(s,X_{s}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}})=Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s),\ a.s.,\ \ \ \forall s\geq t.

Then in view of (
97
) and (
98
), we have

(99)

\begin{split}&u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})=u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})-\mathbb{E}u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}})+\mathbb{E}u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,X_{t+h}^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}})-u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})\\
&\ \ \ =-\mathbb{E}\int_{t}^{t+h}\Big[\partial_{\omega_{r}}u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r})b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\omega_{r}}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r})\sigma^{2}(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})\Big]dr\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ -\mathbb{E}\int_{t}^{t+h}f(r,X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}_{r},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(r))dr.\end{split}

Divide by
h
both sides of the above identity and take
h
to zero, we have

(100)

\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)=-\partial_{\omega}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{2}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})\sigma^{2}(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-f(t,\gamma_{t},u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)).

To build a solution to (
90
), we only need to show
(b,\sigma)(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})
converges to
(b,\sigma)(\gamma_{t})
, and
(I,\partial_{\omega},\partial_{\omega}^{2},\partial_{t})u^{\varepsilon}
also converges to a limit as
\varepsilon
vanishes. In the following we denote by “
\lesssim
” that the left hand is bounded by the right hand side up to a generic constant. Firstly we show that
u^{\varepsilon}
converges to a limit
u
. To this end, we assume that

(A3)

(i)
.
(b,\sigma):\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is locally Lipschitz continuous, i.e. for
\phi=b
or
\sigma,

(101)

|\phi(\omega^{1})-\phi(\omega^{2})|\leq(1+\|\omega^{1}\|^{k}+\|\omega^{2}\|^{k})\|\omega^{1}-\omega^{2}\|,

for some
k\geq 1.
Moreover,
b
and
\sigma
are predictable in the sense of

(102)

(b,\sigma)(\gamma_{t})=(b,\sigma)(\gamma_{t-}),\ \ \ \text{for any }(t,\gamma)\in(0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d},

where
\gamma_{t-}(s)=\gamma_{t}(s-)
for any
s\in(0,T].

(ii)
.
(f,\Phi):[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies assumption (A2), and moreover, for any
(t,\omega,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\{\tau,\tau^{\prime}\}\in[t,T],
we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(\omega)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(\omega)|+|\partial^{2}_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(\omega)-\partial^{2}_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(\omega)|\lesssim(1+\|\omega\|^{k})\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|),

\displaystyle|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,y)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}f(t,\omega,y)|+|\partial^{2}_{\omega_{\tau}}f(t,\omega,y)-\partial^{2}_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}f(t,\omega,y)|\lesssim(1+\|\omega\|^{k}+|y|)\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|),

where
\rho:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is continuous at
0
with
\rho(0)=0.

Example 3.10
.

For a functional
g
on
\mathbb{D}_{T,d}
with property (
102
), we must have
g(\omega_{t})=G(\omega_{t-})
for a path functional
G
and vice versa. The benchmark example is

g(\omega)=\int_{0}^{T}F(r,\omega(r))dr
for a continuous function
F
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
.

Let

(103)

X^{\gamma_{t}}:=X^{0,\gamma_{t}},\ \ \ Y^{\gamma_{t}}:=Y^{0,\gamma_{t}},\ \ \ \text{and }\ u(t,\gamma):=Y^{\gamma_{t}}(t),\ \ \ \forall(t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d}.

Firstly we show that
u^{\varepsilon}
converges to
u
as
\varepsilon
goes to null. In the following we denote by

\text{Osc}(\gamma,t,\varepsilon):=\sup_{u,v\in[t-\varepsilon,t)}|\gamma(u)-\gamma(v)|,\ \ \ \forall\ (t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d}.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose that
(b,\sigma,f,\Phi)
satisfies assumption (A3). Then for any
(t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d},
we have

(104)

\mathbb{E}\|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-X^{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-Y^{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})^{2}\text{Osc}(\gamma,t,\varepsilon)^{2}

In particular, for any
(t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d},

|u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)-u(t,\gamma)|\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})\text{Osc}(\gamma,t,\varepsilon).

Proof.

Note that

X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)-X^{\gamma_{t}}(s)=(b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-b(\gamma_{t}))(s-t)+(\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-\sigma(\gamma_{t}))(W(s)-W(t)).

It follows from the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality that

(105)

\mathbb{E}\|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-X^{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})^{2}\|\gamma_{t-\varepsilon}-\gamma_{t}\|^{2}\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})^{2}\sup_{u,v\in[t-\varepsilon,t)}|\gamma(u)-\gamma(v)|^{2}.

According to Lemma
4.1
, we have the estimate for
\mathbb{E}\|Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-Y^{\gamma_{t}}\|^{2}.

∎

Next we show
u(t,\gamma)
is vertically differentiable. Indeed, in view of
[
46
, Theorem 4.5]
and Remark
4.5
, thanks to the predictable assumption (
102
), we have that
Y^{\gamma_{t}}
is twice vertically differentiable at
(t,\gamma)
following similar argument. Remark that
Y^{\gamma_{t}}
may not be strongly vertically differentiable. Then
u(t,\gamma)
is twice vertically differentiable and its derivatives
(\partial_{\omega}u,\partial_{\omega}^{2}u)(t,\omega)
satisfy

(106)

(\partial_{\omega}u,\partial^{2}_{\omega}u)(t,\gamma)=(\partial_{\gamma_{t}}Y^{\gamma_{t}}(t),\partial^{2}_{\gamma_{t}}Y^{\gamma_{t}}(t)),

where
\partial_{\gamma_{t}}Y^{\gamma_{t}}
and
\partial^{2}_{\gamma_{t}}Y^{\gamma_{t}}
are solutions to (
94
) and (
95
) respectively with
\varepsilon=0.
In conclusion, we have

Lemma 3.12
.

u
is continuously twice vertically differentiable. Moreover, for any
(t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d},

(107)

(\partial_{\omega}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma),\partial_{\omega}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma))\rightarrow(\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma),\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma)),\ \ \ \text{as}\ \varepsilon\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

The first part of this theorem follows from the argument above this lemma. For the second part, in a similar spirit as the proof of (
197
) of Proposition
4.6
, we have

(108)

\|\partial_{\omega}u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma)-\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma)\|\leq\|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-X^{\gamma_{t}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})\text{Osc}(\gamma,t,\varepsilon),

which converges to zero as
\varepsilon
goes to null since
\gamma\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d}.

Then convergence of
\partial_{\omega}^{2}u
follows similarly.

∎

It remains to prove the horizontal differentiability of
u
. To this end, we need the following estimates.

Lemma 3.13
.

For any
\{t,t^{\prime}\}\in(0,T],
and
\{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\}\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d},
we have for any
p\geq 2,

(109)

\displaystyle\|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-X^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}}\leq C(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1}+\|\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{k+1})(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|+|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}),

(110)

\displaystyle\|Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}}\leq C(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1}+\|\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{k+1})(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|+|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}),

where the generic constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon.

Proof.

We only show the first estimate and the second one follows from the first and classical argument as shown in Lemma
4.3
. Without loss of generality, assume
t^{\prime}>t.
Indeed, for
s\leq t,

|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)-X^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}(s)|=|\gamma(s)-\gamma^{\prime}(s)|
. For
s\in[t,t^{\prime})

(111)

\begin{split}|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)-X^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}(s)|&\leq|\gamma(t)-\gamma^{\prime}(s)|+|b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})|(t^{\prime}-t)+|\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})|\ |W_{s}-W_{t}|\\
&\lesssim\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|+(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1})(|t^{\prime}-t|+|W_{s}-W_{t}|).\end{split}

For
s\in[t^{\prime},T],

(112)

\begin{split}&|X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}(s)-X^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}(s)|\\
&\ \ \ \ \leq|\gamma(t)-\gamma^{\prime}(t^{\prime})|+|b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})|(t^{\prime}-t)+|\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})|\ |W_{t^{\prime}}-W_{t}|\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ +(b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-b(\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}))(s-t^{\prime})+(\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-\sigma(\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}-\varepsilon}))|W_{s}-W_{t^{\prime}}|\\
&\ \ \ \ \lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1}+\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{k+1})(|t^{\prime}-t|+|W_{t^{\prime}}-W_{t}|+\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|(1+|W_{s}-W_{t^{\prime}}|)),\end{split}

which implies (
109
) by Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality.

∎

Proposition 3.14
.

Suppose that
(b,\sigma,f,\Phi)
satisfies assumption
(A3)
.
For any
\tau\in(t-\varepsilon,t],
let
\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}
and
\partial^{2}_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}
be solutions of linear BSDEs (
94
) and (
95
) respectively. Then for any
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d},

t,t^{\prime}\in(0,T]
,
\tau\in(t-\varepsilon,t],
and
\tau^{\prime}\in(t^{\prime}-\varepsilon,t^{\prime}],
we have

(113)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-\partial_{\gamma^{\prime}_{\tau^{\prime}}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2},[t^{\prime}\vee t,T]}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C(1+\|\gamma\|^{\ell}+\|\gamma^{\prime}\|^{\ell})(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|+|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)),

(114)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{2}_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}}-\partial^{2}_{\gamma^{\prime}_{\tau^{\prime}}}Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2},[t^{\prime}\vee t,T]}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C(1+\|\gamma\|^{\ell}+\|\gamma^{\prime}\|^{\ell})(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|+|t-t^{\prime}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)),

where
\ell>k+1
and the generic constant
C
is independent of
\varepsilon.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
t\leq t^{\prime}.
In the following we write
(X,X^{\prime},Y,Y^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime})
short for
(X^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},X^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Y^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}},Z^{\varepsilon,\gamma_{t}},Z^{\varepsilon,\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}}).
For any
s\geq t^{\prime},
let

(\delta y(s),\delta z(s)):=(\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Y(s)-\partial_{\gamma^{\prime}_{\tau^{\prime}}}Y^{\prime}(s),\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}Z(s)-\partial_{\gamma^{\prime}_{\tau^{\prime}}}Z^{\prime}(s)),

and it is the solution to linear BSDE

(115)

\begin{split}\delta y(s)=&\ [\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X^{\prime})]+\int_{s}^{T}\Big[\left(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))\right)\\
&+\partial_{y}f(r,X(r),Y(r))\delta y(r)+\left(\partial_{y}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{y}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))\right)Y^{\prime}(r)\Big]dr\\
&-\int_{s}^{T}\delta z(r)dB(r).\end{split}

In view of Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\delta y\|^{2}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},[t^{\prime},T]}\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))|dr\right]^{2}

(116)

\displaystyle\ +\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}|\partial_{y}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{y}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))||Y^{\prime}(r)|dr\right]^{2}.

For the first term on the right hand side of the above inequality, we have

(117)

\begin{split}&\|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(X)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X)-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}\Phi(X^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1}+\|\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{k+1})^{2}(\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)^{2}+\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{2}+|t-t^{\prime}|),\\
\end{split}

where we apply assumption
(A3)
and Lemma
3.13
in the last inequality. Similarly, we have

(118)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}|\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{\omega_{\tau^{\prime}}}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))|dr\right]^{2}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k+1}+\|\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{k+1})^{2}(\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)^{2}+\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{2}+|t-t^{\prime}|).\end{split}

For the last term on the right hand side of (
116
), by the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

(119)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}|\partial_{y}f(r,X(r),Y(r))-\partial_{y}f(r,X^{\prime}(r),Y^{\prime}(r))||Y^{\prime}(r)|dr\right]^{2}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\left[\mathbb{E}[\|X-X^{\prime}\|^{4}+\|Y-Y^{\prime}\|^{4}]\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\mathbb{E}\|Y^{\prime}\|^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{\ell}+\|\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{\ell})(\rho(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)^{2}+\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\|^{2}+|t-t^{\prime}|),\end{split}

with an integer
\ell.
Then inequality (
113
) is implied by the above estimates. (
114
) follows similarly.

∎

Now we are ready to give the main theorem of this subsection. To have the uniqueness of solutions for (
90
), we assume that

(A4)

(b,\sigma)
is Lipschitz on
\mathbb{D}_{T,d},
i.e. for
\phi=b,\sigma,

|\phi(\gamma)-\phi(\gamma^{\prime})|\lesssim\|\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}\|.

Theorem 3.15
.

Suppose assumption
(A3)
holds. Then there exists a classical solution to equation (
90
). Moreover, if
(A4)
also holds for
(b,\sigma),
the solution is unique on
\mathscr{C}^{1,2}_{p}(\mathbb{C}_{T,d}).

Proof.

Firstly we show that
u
given by (
103
) is a classical solution to (
90
). In view of Lemma
3.12
, we need to show
u
is horizontally differentiable. Recall that
u^{\varepsilon}
is given by (
96
). For any
h>0,

(120)

\begin{split}&u(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u(t,\gamma_{t})\\
&\ \ \ =[u(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})]+[u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})]+[u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})-u(t,\gamma_{t})].\\
\end{split}

According to Lemma
3.11
, we have

(121)

|u(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})|\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})\text{Osc}(\gamma_{t},t+h,\varepsilon)=0,

whenever
\varepsilon<h.
Similarly, for the third term on the right hand side of (
120
),

(122)

|u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})-u(t,\gamma_{t})|\lesssim(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|^{k})\text{Osc}(\gamma_{t},t,\varepsilon)

Since
\gamma\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d},
for any
h,
we can choose
\varepsilon
small such that
\text{Osc}(\gamma_{t},t,\varepsilon)=o(h).

Since
u^{\varepsilon}
is horizontally differentiable, in view of (
100
), we have

(123)

\begin{split}&u^{\varepsilon}(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u^{\varepsilon}(t,\gamma_{t})=h\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t})d\lambda\\
=&h\int_{0}^{1}\Big[-\partial_{\omega}u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t})b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\omega}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t})\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})^{2}-f(\gamma_{t},u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t}))\Big].\end{split}

Take (
121
), (
122
) and (
123
) to (
120
), and divide both sides by
h.
Then we have

(124)

\begin{split}\frac{u(t+h,\gamma_{t})-u(t,\gamma_{t})}{h}=&\ o(1)+\int_{0}^{1}\Big[-\partial_{\omega}u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t})b(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\omega}^{2}u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t})\sigma(\gamma_{t-\varepsilon})^{2}\\
&\ -f(t,\gamma_{t},u^{\varepsilon}(t+\lambda h,\gamma_{t}))\Big].\end{split}

Let
h
go to zero, and then
\varepsilon
go to zero. According to Lemma
3.11
and Lemma
3.12
, the right hand side of the above identity converges to
-\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma_{t})b(\gamma_{t})-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma_{t})\sigma(\gamma_{t})^{2}-f(t,\gamma_{t},u(t,\gamma_{t}))
, which implies
u
is horizontally differentiable and
u
satisfy PPDE (
90
).
Now we show the uniqueness of equation (
90
). Suppose that
\bar{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2}_{p}
is a classical solution. Consider a path-dependent SDE

(125)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bar{X}(s):=\gamma(t)+\int_{t}^{s}b(\bar{X}_{r})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(\bar{X}_{r})dB(r),\quad s\geq t>0,\\[5.69054pt]
\bar{X}_{t}=\gamma_{t}.\end{array}\right.

According to assumption
(A4)
, the last equation has a unique solution
\bar{X}
. Applying functional Itô’s formula to
\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s}),
we have

(126)

\begin{split}d\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})=&\ [\partial_{s}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})+\partial_{\omega}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})b(\bar{X}_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\partial^{2}_{\omega}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})\sigma^{2}(\bar{X}_{s})]ds\\
&\ +\ \partial_{\omega}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})\sigma(\bar{X}_{s})dB(s),\\
=&\ -f(s,\bar{X}_{s},\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s}))ds+\partial_{\omega}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})\sigma(\bar{X}_{s})dB(s).\end{split}

Let

(127)

(Y,Z)(s):=(\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s}),\partial_{\omega}\bar{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})\sigma(\bar{X}_{s})),

and it gives a solution to the following BSDE,

(128)

dY(s)=-f(s,\bar{X}_{s},Y(s))ds+Z(s)dB(s),\ \ \ Y(T)=\Phi(\bar{X}_{T}).

According to the well-posedness of the above BSDE, for any classical solution
\tilde{u},

\tilde{u}(s,\bar{X}_{s})=Y(s).

In particular,
\tilde{u}(t,\gamma_{t})=Y(t)=\bar{u}(t,\gamma_{t})
which concludes the uniqueness.

∎

Remark 3.16
.

(i)

For the general case when
f
depends also on
\partial_{\omega}u
, in view of (
61
) and regularity results on
Z
of BSDEs (see
[
40
]
), a smooth solution can be constructed in a similar spirit under the condition that
Z
has continuous paths.

(ii)
For the mean-field equation (
1
), similar as shown in Section
3.2
, we can introduce the following diffusion

(129)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}X(s)=\eta(t)+b_{2}(\mu_{t})(s-t)+\sigma_{2}(\mu_{t})(B(s)-B(t)),\\
X_{t}=\eta_{t}.\end{array}\right.

Similarly as the pure path-dependent case above, we apply the approximation argument with “coefficient frozen” process

(130)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}X^{\varepsilon,\mu_{t}}(s)=\eta(t)+b_{2}(\mu_{t-\varepsilon})(s-t)+\sigma_{2}(\mu_{t-\varepsilon})(B(s)-B(t)),\\
X_{t}=\eta_{t},\end{array}\right.

where
\mathcal{L}(\eta)=\mu.

Under similar assumptions as
(A3)
and
(A4)
with adaptation in a mean-field setting, one can construct a unique classical solution to (
1
).

3.4.
Some typical cases

In view of Remark
2.15
, the path-dependent mean-field equation (
3.2
) involves many interesting special cases. In the following we list some typical ones, where we always assume that
(f,\Phi)
satisfies Assumption
(A2)
and
(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).

(i)

The state-dependent master equation
. Suppose that
(f,\Phi)
has a state-dependent form:

(131)

\displaystyle f(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)=F(t,\omega(t),y,z,\mu(t),\nu),

(132)

\displaystyle\Phi(T,\omega,\mu)=G(\omega(T),\mu(T)),

for functional
F:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
G:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{R}.
In this case, the differentiability of
(f,\Phi)
is equivalent to the differentiability of
(F,G)
in its corresponding domain, and path-dependent equation (
3.2
) has the form

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\gamma,\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma,\mu)]+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\int_{\mathbb{C}_{T,d}}\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u(t,\gamma,\mu,\tilde{\omega})\mu(d\tilde{\omega})\right]\\[5.69054pt]
\ \ \ \ +F(t,\gamma(t),u(t,\gamma,\mu),\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma,\mu),\mu(t),\mathcal{L}_{u(t,W^{\mu},\mu)})=0,\\
\\
u(T,\gamma,\mu)=G(\gamma(T),\mu(T)),\ \ \ (t,\gamma,\mu)\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

Since the corresponding FBSDE is Markovian, we see that
u(t,\gamma,\mu)=U(t,\gamma(t),\mu(t))
for a smooth (indeed
\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1}
in view of Definition
2.14
with obvious adjustment) functional
U:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
thanks to Remark
2.15
. Then we obtain well-posedness of the (state-dependent) master equation considered in
[
9
]
,
[
12
]
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}U(t,a,\lambda)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{a}^{2}U(t,a,\lambda)]+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{\lambda}U(t,a,\lambda,\tilde{a})\lambda(d\tilde{a})\\[5.69054pt]
\ \ \ \ +F(t,a,U(t,a,\lambda),\partial_{a}U(t,a,\lambda),\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{u(t,\xi,\lambda)})=0,\\
\\
U(T,a,\lambda)=G(a,\lambda),\ \ \ \ (t,a,\lambda)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\end{array}\right.

where
\xi
is a random variable on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with law
\lambda.

(ii)

The PPDE
. Suppose that
(f,\Phi)
does not depend on measures:

(143)

\displaystyle f(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)=H(t,\omega,y,z),

(144)

\displaystyle\Phi(T,\omega,\mu)=I(\omega_{T}),

with
H:[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
I:\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}.
Then equation (
3.2
) is written as PPDE,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma)]+H(t,\gamma,u(t,\gamma),\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma))=0,\\
\\
u(T,\gamma)=I(\gamma_{T}),\ \ \ \ (t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

Then Theorem
3.8
recovers the well-posedness of PPDEs shown in
[
46
, Theorem 4.5]
under a stronger assumption in view of the integrability of BSDE (
57
).

(iii)

The measure-dependent master equation
. Suppose that
(f,\Phi)
does not depend on the path/state variable and has the following structure

(149)

\displaystyle f(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)=J(t,y,\mu),

(150)

\displaystyle\Phi(T,\omega,\mu)=K(\mu_{T}),

where
J:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{P}^{C}_{2}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
K:\mathcal{P}^{C}_{2}\mapsto\mathbb{R}.
In this case our path-dependent mean-field equation is reduced to

(151)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\int_{\mathbb{C}_{T,d}}\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u(t,\mu,\tilde{\omega})\mu(d\tilde{\omega})\right]+J(t,u(t,\mu),\mu)=0,\\
\\
u(T,\mu)=K(\mu_{T}),\ \ \ \ (t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}.\end{array}\right.

Such form of master equation is introduced in
[
55
]
for a closed-loop control problem with control being the form of
\alpha_{t}=\alpha(t,\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}}).

(iv)

Path-state mixed cases
. Suppose that
(f,\Phi)
has the following form

(152)

\displaystyle f(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)=L(t,\omega,y,z,\mu(t)),

(153)

\displaystyle\Phi(T,\omega,\mu)=M(\omega_{T},\mu(T)),

where
L:[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
M:\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
. Then we have a unique smooth solution for the following mean-field equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\gamma,\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}u(t,\gamma,\mu)]+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\int_{\mathbb{C}_{T,d}}\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}u(t,\gamma,\mu,\tilde{\omega})\mu(d\tilde{\omega})\right]\\[5.69054pt]
\ \ \ \ +L(t,\gamma,u(t,\gamma,\mu),\partial_{\omega}u(t,\gamma,\mu),\mu(t))=0,\\
\\
u(T,\gamma,\mu)=M(\gamma_{T},\mu(T)),\ \ \ (t,\gamma,\mu)\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

In view of the corresponding FBSDE (
56
), we see that
u(t,\gamma,\mu)=U(t,\gamma,\mu(t))
for a functional
U:[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{R}.
Since
u\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2,1,1},

U
is the unique classical solution to the master equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}U(t,\gamma,\lambda)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{\omega}^{2}U(t,\gamma,\lambda)]+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{\lambda}U(t,\gamma,\lambda,\tilde{a})\lambda(d\tilde{a})\right]\\[5.69054pt]
\ \ \ \ +L(t,\gamma,U(t,\gamma,\lambda),\partial_{\omega}U(t,\gamma,\lambda),\mu(t))=0,\\
\\
U(T,\gamma,\lambda)=M(\gamma_{T},\lambda),\ \ \ (t,\gamma,\lambda)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, if
(f,\Phi)
has the the following structure

(164)

\displaystyle f(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu)=N(t,\omega(t),y,z,\mu,\nu)\quad\quad\text{and}

(165)

\displaystyle\Phi(T,\omega,\mu)=P(\omega(T),\mu_{T})

for some functionals
N:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
P:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\mapsto\mathbb{R},
then
u(t,\omega,\mu)=V(t,\omega(t),\mu_{t})
for a functional
V:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\to\mathbb{R}.
Then,
V
is the unique smooth solution to the master equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}V(t,a,\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\ [\partial_{a}^{2}V(t,a,\mu)]+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\int_{\mathbb{C}_{T,d}}\partial_{\tilde{\omega}}\partial_{\mu}V(t,x,\mu,\tilde{\omega})\mu(d\tilde{\omega})\right]\\[5.69054pt]
\ \ \ \ +N(t,a,V(t,a,\mu),\partial_{a}V(t,a,\mu),\mu,\mathcal{L}_{V(t,W^{\mu}(t),\mu)})=0,\\
\\
V(T,a,\mu)=P(a,\mu_{T}),\ \ \ (t,a,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}.\end{array}\right.

(v)

A non-smooth case
.
For any
t_{0}\in(0,T)
and
F\in C_{b}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),
consider PPDE

(171)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\partial^{2}_{\omega}u(t,\omega)\right]=0,\\[5.69054pt]
u(T,\omega)=F(\omega(t_{0})),\ \ \ (t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

In this case we have

\partial_{\omega_{t}}F(\omega(t_{0}))=DF(\omega(t_{0}))1_{[0,t_{0}]}(t),\quad\forall(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d},

which is not continuous on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d}
. Therefore,
(A2)
is not satisfied and the preceding PPDE has no smooth solution. In particular, when
F(x)=ax
for some
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
by resolvability of the corresponding BSDE, the functional
u(t,\omega):=a\omega(t\wedge t_{0}),(t,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d},
is the unique viscosity solution in the sense of
[
23
]
.

In a similar way, for any
(F,G)\in C_{b}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times C_{b}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the path-dependent master equation

(172)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u(t,\mu)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\partial_{\omega^{\prime}}\partial_{\mu}u(t,\mu,\omega^{\prime})\right]=0,\\[5.69054pt]
u(T,\mu)=F\left(\mathbb{E}^{\mu}[G(W(t_{0}))]\right),\ \ \ (t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}^{C}\end{array}\right.

has no smooth solution. In particular, if
\Phi(T,\mu)=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}[aW(t_{0})],

u(t,\mu):=\mathbb{E}^{\mu}[aW(t\wedge t_{0})]
is the unique viscosity solution of equation (
172
) in the sense of
[
55
]
.

4.
Differentiability of solutions of path-dependent mean-field BSDEs

In the following, for any process
(X,Y,Z)
on the probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P)
, we denote by
(\tilde{X},\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})
an independent copy of
(X,Y,Z)
, which means that
(\tilde{X},\tilde{Y},\tilde{Z})
is defined in an independent probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{P})
and has the same law as
(X,Y,Z).
The following linear mean-field BSDEs and estimates are frequently used in subsequent discussions. Different from a classical linear BSDE, all linear coefficients are not necessarily bounded. For simplicity, we only address the one-dimensional case. Similar assertions in this section are still true in the multi-dimensional case.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{T})
and
t\in[0,T)
. Suppose that
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
is bounded,
c\in\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, and
h
is a real valued progressively measurable process such that
\int_{t}^{T}|h(r)|dr\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{T}).
For any
(r,x)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{k},

g(\cdot,x)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T])
and
g(r,\cdot)
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous:

\sup_{r\in[t,T]}|g(r,x)-g(r,y)|\leq L|x-y|,\quad\forall y\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\ P\text{-}a.s.

for a constant
L.
Then the following linear mean-field BSDE:
s\in[t,T],

(173)

Y(s)=\xi+\int_{s}^{T}\Big(\alpha(r)Y(r)+\beta(r)Z(r)+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{Y}(r)]+h(r)\Big)dr-\int_{s}^{T}Z(r)dB(r),

with
(\tilde{c},\tilde{Y})
being an independent copy of
(c,Y),
has a unique solution
(Y,Z)\in\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T])\times\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover, we have

(174)

\|(Y,Z)\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+||\int_{t}^{T}|h(r)|dr||^{2}_{L^{2}})e^{C(\|c\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}+\|g(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}})}

for a constant
C
depending on the bound of
\alpha,\beta
and
L.

In particular, if
g
is uniformly bounded, we have

(175)

\|(Y,Z)\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+||\int_{t}^{T}|h(r)|dr||^{2}_{L^{2}}).

Remark 4.2
.

Since neither of
g(t,x)
and
g(r,c(r))
is bounded or uniformly integrable for any
c(r)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{k})
, the well-posedness of the mean-field BSDE is not an immediate consequence of existing works such as
[
8
]
.

Proof.

For any
y\in\mathbb{H}^{2},
consider the following classical linear BSDE

(176)

Y(s)=\xi+\int_{s}^{T}\Big(\alpha(r)Y(r)+\beta(r)Z(r)+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{y}(r)]+h(r)\Big)dr-\int_{s}^{T}Z(r)dB(r),

where
(\tilde{c},\tilde{y})
is an independent copy of
(c,y)
.
To prove that it is well-posed on
[t,T]
, we only need to show

(177)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\Big|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{y}(r)]\Big|dr\right]^{2}<\infty.

Indeed, by the uniformly Lipschitz continuity of
g
, we have

(178)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Big|\int_{t}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{y}(r)]|dr\Big|^{2}\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|g(r,0)\tilde{y}(r)|dr+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{c}(r)\tilde{y}(r)|dr\Big]^{2}\\
&\ \ \leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t}^{T}|g(r,0)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{y}(r)]|dr\Big]^{2}+C\Big[\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[|\tilde{c}(r)\tilde{y}(r)|]dr\Big]^{2}\\
&\ \ \leq C\Big[(\mathbb{E}\int_{t}^{T}|g(r,0)|^{2}dr)(\int_{t}^{T}[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{y}(r)|]^{2}dr)+(\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{c}(r)|^{2}dr)(\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|\tilde{y}(r)|^{2}dr)\Big]\\
&\ \ \leq C\Big[\|g(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\|y\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\|c\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\|y\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\Big]\leq C(\|g(\cdot,0)\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\|c\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2})\|y\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},\end{split}

where we have used in the third inequality the Hölder inequality to integrals over
[t,T]
and
[t,T]\times\Omega
. Then for any
y\in\mathbb{H}^{2},
BSDE (
176
) has a unique solution
(Y,Z)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}
. The solution mapping
\Phi:y\mapsto Y
defines a transformation on
\mathbb{H}^{2}
, and turns out to be a contraction under the following equivalent norm

(179)

\|Y\|^{2}:=\mathbb{E}\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}(\|g(r,0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}})dr}|Y(s)|^{2}ds,\quad Y\in\mathbb{H}^{2}

with
A
being a constant to be determined later. In fact,
take any
y^{(j)}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
and denote by
(Y^{(j)},Z^{(j)})
the corresponding solution of classical BSDE (
176
), with
j=1,2
. Set
(\Delta Y,\Delta Z):=(Y^{(1)}-Y^{(2)},Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)})
,
\Delta y:=y^{(1)}-y^{(2)}
, and
f(r):=\|g(r,0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|c(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}
.
Applying Itô’s formula to
e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y(s)|^{2}
on
s\in[t,T]
, we have

\begin{split}-e^{At-\int_{t}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y(t)|^{2}&=\int_{t}^{T}(A+f(s))e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y(s)|^{2}ds\\
&\ \ \ \ +2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Delta Yd(\Delta Y)+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Z|^{2}ds.\end{split}

Therefore,

\displaystyle e^{At-\int_{t}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y(t)|^{2}+\int_{t}^{T}(A+f(s))e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y(s)|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Z(s)|^{2}dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad 2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Delta Y[\alpha\Delta Y+\beta\Delta Z+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(s,\tilde{c}(s))\Delta\tilde{y}]]ds

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Delta Y\Delta ZdW(s)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\quad C\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y|^{2}ds+C\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y|^{2}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Z|^{2}ds+2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y||g(s,0)|\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y|\|c\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}}ds-2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Delta Y\Delta ZdB(s)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\quad C\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Y|^{2}ds+\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}|\Delta Z|^{2}ds

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Delta Y\Delta ZdB(s)+2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}(|\Delta Y||g(s,0)|\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}})ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}(|\Delta Y|^{2}\|c\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})ds.

Taking expectation on both sides of the above inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}(A-C+f(s))e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\|\Delta Y(s)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 2\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta Y||g(s,0)|]\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}(\|\Delta Y\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|c\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Big[\Big(\mathbb{E}[|\Delta Y||g(s,0)|]\Big)^{2}+\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big]ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}(\|\Delta Y\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|c\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\Big(\|g(s,0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|c\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\Big)\|\Delta Y\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{T}e^{As-\int_{s}^{T}f(r)dr}\|\Delta\tilde{y}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds.

Therefore, choosing a sufficiently large number
A
such that
A-C>1
, we obtain a contraction and then the well-posedness of (
173
).

Now BSDE (
173
) can be written as the following classical BSDE

(180)

Y(s)=\xi+\int_{s}^{T}\Big(\alpha(r)Y(r)+\beta(r)Z(r)+h^{\prime}(r)\Big)dr-\int_{s}^{T}Z(r)dB(r),

with

h^{\prime}(r)=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{Y}(r)]+h(r).

Thus it is standard that

(181)

\begin{split}\|Y\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{2}+\|Z\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}&\leq C(\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\int_{t}^{T}|h^{\prime}(r)|dr\|^{2}_{L^{2}})\\
&\leq C(\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\int_{t}^{T}|h(r)|dr\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\int_{t}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{Y}(r)]|dr\|^{2}_{L^{2}}).\end{split}

Furthermore, similar to the proof of inequality (
178
), we have

(182)

\|\int_{t}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[g(r,\tilde{c}(r))\tilde{Y}(r)]|dr\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C[\int_{t}^{T}\|g(r,0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|Y\|^{2}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},[t,r]}dr+\int_{t}^{T}\|c(r)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|Y\|^{2}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},[t,r]}dr].

Then, using Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain the desired estimate (
174
).

∎

To study the differentiability of corresponding FBSDEs, for any
(t,\eta)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{M}^{D}_{2}
, we denote by
(Y^{\eta_{t}},Z^{\eta_{t}})
the solution of the following path-dependent mean-field BSDE, for
s\in[t,T],

(183)

Y(s)=\Phi(B_{T}^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta_{t}}})+\int_{s}^{T}f(B_{r}^{\eta_{t}},Y(r),Z(r),\mathcal{L}_{B_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y(r)})dr-\int_{s}^{T}Z(r)dB(r).

On the other hand, for any
\gamma\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d},
let
(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}})
solve the associated path-dependent BSDE: for
s\in[t,T],

(184)

\mathcal{Y}{(s)}=\Phi(B_{T}^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta_{t}}})+\int_{s}^{T}\!\!\!f(B_{r}^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{Y}(r),\mathcal{Z}(r),\mathcal{L}_{B_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}(r)})dr-\int_{s}^{T}\mathcal{Z}(r)dB(r).

Note that under Assumption
(H0)
, the functional

(185)

\hat{f}(r,y,z,\nu):=f(B_{r}^{\eta_{t}},y,z,\mathcal{L}_{B_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\nu),\quad(r,y,z,\nu)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),

is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in
(y,z)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. According to
[
13
, Theorem 4.23]
, BSDE (
183
) is well posed with
(Y^{\eta_{t}},Z^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}).
Then (
184
) is a well-defined classical BSDE with
(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}})\in\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p}
for any
p\geq 1.
In the following, we write
\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r}:=(B_{r}^{\eta_{t}},Y^{\eta_{t}}(r),Z^{\eta_{t}}(r)),\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r}:=(B_{r}^{\gamma_{t}},Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(r),Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(r))
,
\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r}}:=(\mathcal{L}_{B_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}(r)})
and
(Y,Z):=(Y(t),Z(t))
if no confusion is raised.
Then we have the following basic estimates for BSDEs (
183
) and (
184
).

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume that
(\Phi,f)
satisfies
(H0)
. For any
K>0
and

(\gamma,\eta),(\gamma^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{M}_{2}^{D}
such that
|||\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}|||,|||\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}_{t}}|||\leq K,
we have for any
p\geq 1,

(186)

\displaystyle\|(Y^{\eta_{t}},Z^{\eta_{t}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}\leq C(1+\|\eta_{t}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}),

(187)

\displaystyle\|(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p}}\leq C_{p}(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|+\|\eta_{t}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}),

(188)

\displaystyle\|(Y^{\eta_{t}}-Y^{\eta^{\prime}_{t}},Z^{\eta_{t}}-Z^{\eta^{\prime}_{t}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}\leq C_{K}\|\eta_{t}-\eta^{\prime}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\quad\quad\text{and }

(189)

\displaystyle\|(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}-Y^{\gamma^{\prime}_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}-Z^{\gamma^{\prime}_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p}}\leq C_{K,p}(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t}\|+W_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}_{t}})),

where
(C,C_{p})
does not depend on
(\gamma,\eta)
, and
(C_{K},C_{K,p})
does not depend on
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
.

Remark 4.4
.

According to inequality (
189
),
(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}})
and
(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}})
are indistinguishable if
\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}=\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}_{t}},
which implies the following definition is well-posed

(190)

(Y^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}):=(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}).

The proof of Lemma
4.3
is rather lengthy, which follows from Lemma
4.1
and is left in the appendix.

4.1.
First-order differentiability

In this subsection we assume that
(H1)
holds for
(\Phi,f).

For any
(\gamma,\eta)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{M}^{D}_{2},
we consider the first order differentiability of
Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}=Y^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}
with respect to
\gamma_{t}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}.
For the differentiability in
\gamma_{t},

let

(191)

\begin{split}&\hat{f}(\omega_{s},y,z):=f(\omega_{s},y,z,\mathcal{L}_{B_{s}^{\eta_{t}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta_{t}}(s)}),\\
&\hat{\Phi}(\omega_{T}):=\Phi(\omega_{T},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta_{t}}}),\ \forall(s,\omega,y,z)\in[t,T]\times\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\end{split}

and then the solution
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}{(s)}
to equation (
184
) solves the following path-dependent BSDE

(192)

\hat{Y}{(s)}=\hat{\Phi}(B_{T}^{\gamma_{t}})+\int_{s}^{T}\hat{f}(B^{\gamma_{t}}_{r},\hat{Y}(r),\hat{Z}(r))dr-\int_{s}^{T}\hat{Z}(r)dB(r).

Define

\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,{\gamma}):=Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(t).
If
f
and
\Phi
are regular enough, according to
[
46
, Theorem 4.5]
,
\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,{\gamma})
is twice vertically differentiable at
(t,\gamma),
and moreover for any
s\geq t,

(193)

\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(s,B^{\gamma_{t}})=Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(s),\ \ \ \partial_{\gamma_{t}}\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(s,B^{\gamma_{t}})=Z^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(s).

Furthermore,
\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,\gamma)
is the unique solution to the following semilinear PPDE

(194)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\text{Tr}\left[\partial_{\omega}^{2}\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,\gamma)\right]+\hat{f}(\gamma_{t},\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,{\gamma}),\partial_{\omega}\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(t,{\gamma}))=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\hat{u}_{\eta_{t}}(T,\gamma)=\hat{\Phi}(\gamma),\ \ \ (t,\gamma)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{C}_{T,d}.\end{array}\right.

In the following, we denote by
\partial_{(t,\omega,y,z,\mu,\nu,{\omega_{\tau}},{\mu_{\tau}})}f
the derivative vector

(\partial_{t}f,\partial_{\omega}f,\partial_{y}f,\partial_{z}f,\partial_{\mu}f,\partial_{\nu}f,\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f,\partial_{\mu_{\tau}}f).

Remark 4.5
.

Assume that
\Phi:\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is twice continuously strongly vertically differentiable and satisfies the following locally Lipschitz continuous condition: for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\phi=\Phi,\partial_{\omega_{t}}\Phi,\partial_{\omega_{t}}^{2}\Phi
,

(195)

|\phi(\omega_{T})-\phi(\omega^{\prime}_{T})|\leq C(1+\|\omega_{T}|^{k}+\|\omega^{\prime}_{T}\|^{k})\|\omega_{T}-\omega^{\prime}_{T}\|,\quad\forall\ (\omega,\omega^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d}^{2}.

Then, the main result
[
46
, Theorem 4.5]
is still true.
For the reader’s convenience, the proof is sketched in the appendix, using our partial Itô-Dupire formula.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
(f,\Phi)
satisfy Assumption
(H1)
. Then for any
\tau\leq t,

(Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(s),Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}(s))
is strongly vertically differentiable at
(\tau,t,\gamma)
. The derivative
(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}})\in\mathbb{S}^{p}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{H}^{p}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}),\ \forall\ p\geq 1
, is the unique solution to BSDE

(196)

\begin{split}\mathcal{Y}(s)=\ &\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi(B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}})+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})dr+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Y}(r)dr\\
&+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Z}(r)dr-\int_{s}^{T}\mathcal{Z}(r)dB(r),\ \ s\in[t,T].\end{split}

Furthermore, since
(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}})
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, we have that for any
K>0,
and any
(\gamma,\eta),(\gamma^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{T,d}\times\mathbb{M}_{2}^{D}\text{ such that }|||\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}|||,|||\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}_{t}}|||\leq K,

(197)

\begin{split}&\|(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}},\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p}}<C_{p},\\
&\|(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y^{\gamma^{\prime}_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}},\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z^{\gamma^{\prime}_{t},\eta^{\prime}_{t}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p}}<C_{K,p}(\|\gamma_{t}-\gamma^{\prime}_{t}\|+W_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}_{t}})),\end{split}

for some positive constants
C_{p}
and
C_{K,p}
.

Proof.

In view of Assumption
(H1)
and Lemma
4.1
,
we see that equation (
196
) has a unique solution
(\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y,\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z)\in\mathbb{S}^{p}\times\mathbb{H}^{p},\ \forall p\geq 1.

Here, we consider the one-dimensional case for simplicity. For any
h>0,
recall that
\gamma^{\tau,h}=\gamma+h1_{[\tau,T]}
. Set

(198)

\begin{split}&\gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma^{\tau,h},\quad\Delta_{h}Y:=\frac{1}{h}(Y^{\prime}-Y):=\frac{1}{h}(Y^{\gamma^{\tau,h}_{t},\eta_{t}}-Y^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}),\quad\text{and}\\
&\quad\Delta_{h}Z:=\frac{1}{h}(Z^{\prime}-Z):=\frac{1}{h}(Z^{\gamma^{\tau,h}_{t},\eta_{t}}-Z^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}}).\end{split}

Then we know that
(\Delta_{h}Y,\Delta_{h}Z)
solves the following BSDE

\begin{split}\Delta_{h}Y(s)&=\frac{1}{h}(\Phi^{\prime}-\Phi)+\frac{1}{h}\int_{s}^{T}[f(\Theta^{\gamma^{\prime},\eta}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta}_{r}})-f(\Theta^{\gamma,\eta}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta}_{r}})]dr-\int_{s}^{T}\Delta_{h}Z(r)dB(r)\\
&=:\Delta_{h}\Phi+\int_{s}^{T}\Big(a_{r}\Delta_{h}Y(r)+b_{r}\Delta_{h}Z(r)+\Delta_{h}f\Big)dr-\int_{s}^{T}\Delta_{h}Z(r)dB(r),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}:=\Phi(B^{\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}}),\ \ \Phi:=\Phi(B^{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}}),\ \ \Delta_{h}\Phi:=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\gamma_{\tau}}\Phi(B^{\gamma^{\tau,h\theta}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}})\ d\theta,

\displaystyle a_{r}:=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y}{f}(B^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{r},Y+\theta(Y^{\prime}-Y),Z^{\prime},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta}_{r}})\ d\theta,\ \ b_{r}:=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{z}{f}(B^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{r},Y,Z+\theta(Z^{\prime}-Z),\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta}_{r}})\ d\theta,

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta_{h}f:=\frac{1}{h}f(B^{\omega}_{r},Y,Z,\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}_{r}},\mathcal{L}_{Y})\Big|_{\omega=\gamma}^{\omega=\gamma^{\prime}}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(B^{\gamma^{\tau,h\theta}},Y,Z,\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}_{r}},\mathcal{L}_{Y})\ d\theta.

Then
(\delta Y,\delta Z):=(\Delta_{h}Y-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y,\Delta_{h}Z-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z)
satisfies BSDE

\begin{split}\delta Y(s)=&\ (\Delta_{h}\Phi-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi)+\int_{s}^{T}\left(a_{r}\delta Y+b_{r}\delta Z+(\Delta_{h}f-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}}))\right)dr\\
\ \ &+\int_{s}^{T}[(a_{r}-\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}}))\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Y+(b_{r}-\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}}))\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}Z]dr-\int_{s}^{T}\delta ZdB(r).\end{split}

According to standard estimate for BSDEs (or Lemma
4.1
for
p=2
) and Lemma
4.3
, we have

\begin{split}\|\delta Y\|_{\mathbb{S}^{p}}^{p}+\|\delta Z\|_{\mathbb{H}^{p}}^{p}&\leq C\|\Delta_{h}\Phi-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}\Phi\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+\|\int_{t}^{T}|\Delta_{h}f-\partial_{\omega_{\tau}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})|dr\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+O(|h|)\\
&\leq O(|h|),\end{split}

and thus the strongly vertical differentiability.

∎

To show the differentiability of
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}
with respect to
\eta_{t},
we follow a similar argument as in the state-dependent case for SDEs made in
[
9
]
. Firstly we show that
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}
is Gâteaux differentiable in
\eta_{t}
in the sense of (
25
) and Remark
2.9
. To this end, we need to prove that for any
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{t},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\eta_{t}^{\lambda\xi}:=\eta_{t}+\lambda\xi 1_{[t,T]}
,
\lambda>0,
the following limit exits in
\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}),

(199)

\partial_{\eta}Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(\xi):=\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\lambda}(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}^{\lambda\xi}}-Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}).

Then we show that
\partial_{\eta}Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(\cdot):L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{t},\mathbb{R}^{d})\mapsto\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a bounded linear operator, and moreover, it is continuous in the following sense: for any
\zeta\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{t},\mathbb{R}^{d}),

\partial_{\eta}Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}+\zeta 1_{[t,T]}}
converges to
\partial_{\eta}Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}
in the sense of operators as
\zeta
goes to zero. In view of Remark
2.6
, we see that
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}
is Fréchet (vertically) differentiable in the sense of (
24
) and Remark
2.9
. To this end, consider the following linear BSDE

(200)

\begin{split}\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}(s)&=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]+\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]dr\\
&\ \ \ +\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dr+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Z}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dr\\
&\ \ \ +\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi}+\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\tilde{\xi}})(r)]dr\\
&\ \ \ -\int_{s}^{T}\mathcal{Z}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dB(r),\quad s\in[t,T].\end{split}

Here,
(\tilde{B},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}},\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\tilde{\xi}})
is an independent copy of
(B,\eta,\xi,Y^{\eta_{t}},\partial_{\omega_{t}}Y^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}|_{\gamma=\eta},\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi})
,
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi}
satisfies the following linear mean-field BSDE

(201)

\begin{split}\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi}(s)&=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]+\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}_{r})\tilde{\xi}]dr\\
&\ \ \ +\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Y}^{{\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dr+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\mathcal{Z}^{{\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dr\\
&\ \ \ +\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi}+\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\tilde{\xi}})(r)]dr\\
&\ \ \ -\int_{s}^{T}\mathcal{Z}^{{\eta_{t}},\xi}(r)dB(r),\quad s\in[t,T].\end{split}

Lemma 4.7
.

For any
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{t},\mathbb{R}^{d}),

there exits a unique solution
(\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\eta_{t},\xi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T])\times\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})
to BSDE (
201
). Moreover,
(\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\eta_{t},\xi})
is linear in
\xi
, and we have

(202)

\|(\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\eta_{t},\xi})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}

for some constant
C
.

Proof.

By Lipschitz continuity of
(\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi,\partial_{\mu_{t}}f),
we have

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B_{T}^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}_{T}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{T}),\ \ \ \tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}_{r})\tilde{\xi}]\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{r}).

Since
f
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in
(y,z),

\partial_{(y,z)}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})
is uniformly bounded.
Set
g(r,x):=\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},x)
. In view of Lemma
4.3
and Assumption
(H1)
, we see that
g(\cdot,0)\in\mathbb{H}^{2}.

Then by Lemma
4.1
, to show the well-posedness of linear mean-field BSDE (
201
),
we only need to check the following

\int_{t}^{T}\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi})]\right|dr\in L^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{T}).

Let

(203)

F_{2}(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu):=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu,\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}(r))(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}(r)\tilde{\xi})].

Then by Lipschitz continuity of
\partial_{\nu}f
and Proposition
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle F_{2}(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu)

\displaystyle\ \ =\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}}[\partial_{\nu}f(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu,\tilde{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}(r))(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}(r))]\Big|_{\gamma_{t}=\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\tilde{\xi}\Big]

\displaystyle\ \ \leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}}[|\tilde{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}||\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}(r)|]\left|{}_{\gamma_{t}=\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\right.\tilde{\xi}\Big]+\partial_{\nu}f(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu,0)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}}[\tilde{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}(r)|]\left|{}_{\gamma_{t}=\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\right.\tilde{\xi}\Big]

\displaystyle\ \ \leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}}[(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|)]\Big|_{\gamma_{t}=\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\tilde{\xi}\right]+C\partial_{\nu}f(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu,0)

\displaystyle\ \ \leq C+C\partial_{\nu}f(t,x,y,z,\mu,\nu,0),

where we have applied Lemmas
4.3
in the second inequality. Then according to Lemma
4.3
again, we have
\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r}},0)\in\mathbb{H}^{2},
and thus the well-posedness of (
201
). For inequality (
202
), similar to the proof of Lemma
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{2}+\|\mathcal{Z}^{\eta_{t},\xi}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]|^{2}+\int_{s}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\int_{s}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi})|^{2}dr\Big]

\displaystyle\ \ \leq C\Big((\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\|\ |\tilde{\xi}|)^{2}+\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathbb{E}|\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\eta_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},0)|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{s}^{T}|\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},0)|^{2}dr+\mathbb{E}\int_{s}^{T}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi}]|^{2}dr

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{s}^{T}|\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},0)|^{2}dr\Big)\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

∎

Since BSDE (
201
) is well-posed, so is BSDE (
200
).
In conclusion, we have

Corollary 4.8
.

There exits a unique solution
(\mathcal{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t},\xi})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T])\times\mathbb{H}^{2}([t,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})
to BSDE (
200
). Moreover,

(204)

(\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\eta_{t},\xi})=(\mathcal{Y}^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t},\xi})|_{\gamma=\eta}.

Lemma 4.9
.

The map

\xi\mapsto\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}
is a bounded linear operator from
L^{2}({\mathcal{F}_{t}},\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T])
. Moreover, it is the Gâteaux derivative of
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}
with respect to
\eta_{t}
in the following sense

(205)

\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}=\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\lambda}(Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}^{\lambda\xi}}-Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}})\quad\text{strongly in $\mathbb{S}^{2}([t,T])$}.

In particular,
\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}(s)
is the Gâteaux derivative of
Y^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}(s)
in the sense of (
25
).

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{Y}^{\eta_{t},\xi}
is linear in
\xi,
we see that
(\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi},\ \mathcal{Z}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi})
is also linear in
\xi
. Moreover, we have the following estimate

(206)

\|(\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi},\mathcal{Z}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}.

Therefore, we have the first assertion.

In the following, we omit the fixed subscript
t
and write
(Y,Z):=(Y(r),Z(r))
if no confusion raised. Besides, the constant
C
may change from line to line. Set

(207)

\begin{split}&\Delta_{\lambda}Y:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(Y^{\gamma,\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-Y^{\gamma,\eta}),\ \ \Delta_{\lambda}Z:=\frac{1}{\lambda}(Z^{\gamma,\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-Z^{\gamma,\eta}),\quad\quad\text{and }\\
&\quad\Delta_{\lambda}\Phi:=\frac{1}{\lambda}[\Phi(B_{T}^{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}})-\Phi(B_{T}^{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{B_{T}^{\eta}})].\end{split}

Then according to Lemma
4.3
, we have

(208)

\|\Delta_{\lambda}Y\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+\|\Delta_{\lambda}Z\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}\leq C\frac{1}{\lambda}\|\eta_{t}^{\lambda\xi}-\eta_{t}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}.

In view of BSDE (
184
), we see that
(\Delta_{\lambda}Y,\Delta_{\lambda}Z)
satisfies the following linear mean-field BSDE

(209)

\begin{split}\Delta_{\lambda}Y&=\Delta_{\lambda}\Phi+\int_{s}^{T}\left[\alpha(r)\Delta_{\lambda}Y+\beta(r)\Delta_{\lambda}Z+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{g}(r)\frac{1}{\lambda}(\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}^{\lambda\tilde{\xi}}}-\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}})]+\Delta_{\lambda}f\right]dr\\
&\quad-\int_{s}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}ZdB(r),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\alpha(r):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{y}{f}(B^{\gamma}_{r},Y^{\gamma,\eta}+\theta(Y^{\gamma,\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-Y^{\gamma,\eta}),Z^{\gamma,\eta^{\lambda\xi}},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}_{r}})d\theta,

\displaystyle\beta(r):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{z}{f}(B^{\gamma}_{r},Y^{\gamma,\eta},Z^{\gamma,\eta}+\theta(Z^{\gamma,\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-Z^{\gamma,\eta}),\mathcal{L}_{\Theta^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}_{r}})d\theta,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(r):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\gamma,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta}+\theta(Y^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-Y^{\eta})},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}+\theta(\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}^{\lambda\tilde{\xi}}}-\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}))d\theta,\quad\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\Delta_{\lambda}f(r):=\frac{1}{\lambda}[f(\Theta^{\gamma,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}_{r}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta}})-f(\Theta^{\gamma,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}_{r}},\mathcal{L}_{Y^{\eta}})].

According to estimate (
188
) in Lemma
4.3
, we have

(210)

\|\Delta_{\lambda}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}:=\|\frac{1}{\lambda}(\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}^{\lambda\tilde{\xi}}}-\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}})\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq C\frac{1}{\lambda}\|\eta_{t}^{\lambda\xi}-\eta_{t}\|_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}.

Then, in view of Assumption
(H1)
, we have

(211)

\|\int_{t}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{g}(r)\Delta_{\lambda}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}]dr\|_{L^{2}}+\|\int_{t}^{T}\Delta_{\lambda}fdr\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}.

Thus BSDE (
209
) has a unique solution
(\Delta_{\lambda}Y,\Delta_{\lambda}Z)
, and then
(\Delta_{\lambda}Y-\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi},\Delta_{\lambda}Z-\mathcal{Z}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi})
is the unique solution of the following BSDE

\displaystyle Y(s)=

\displaystyle\ (\Delta_{\lambda}\Phi-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}])+\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})Ydr

\displaystyle\ +\int_{s}^{T}\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})Z(r)dr+\int_{s}^{T}(\Delta_{\lambda}f-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}])dr

\displaystyle\ +\int_{s}^{T}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\Delta_{\lambda}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}-\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi}-\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\tilde{\xi}})]dr

\displaystyle\ +\int_{t}^{T}R_{1}(r)dr-\int_{s}^{T}ZdB(r)

with

\displaystyle R_{1}(r):=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha(r)-\partial_{y}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\right)\Delta_{\lambda}Y+\left(\beta(r)-\partial_{z}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}})\right)\Delta_{\lambda}Z

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[(\tilde{g}(r)-\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}}))\Delta_{\lambda}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}\right].

Since
\partial_{(y,z)}f
is bounded, from the standard estimate for solutions of BSDEs, we have

(212)

\|\Delta_{\lambda}Y-\mathcal{Y}^{{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}},\xi}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq C(\|A_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|A_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|A_{3}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|A_{4}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})

with

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Delta_{\lambda}\Phi-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}],\quad A_{2}:=\int_{t}^{T}|R_{1}(r)|dr,

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle:=\int_{t}^{T}\left|(\Delta_{\lambda}f-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}])\right|dr,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle A_{4}

\displaystyle:=\int_{t}^{T}\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\nu}f(\Theta^{\gamma_{t},\eta_{t}}_{r},\mathcal{L}_{\Theta_{r}^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})(\Delta_{\lambda}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}}-\partial_{\omega_{t}}\tilde{Y}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\mathcal{L}_{\eta_{t}}}\tilde{\xi}-\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t},\tilde{\xi}})(r)]\right|dr.

For
A_{1},
according to the Lipschitz continuity of
\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi,
we have

(213)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}|A_{1}|^{2}=&\ \mathbb{E}\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta}+\theta(B^{\eta^{\lambda\xi}}-B^{\eta})},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}}+\theta(\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}^{\lambda\tilde{\xi}}}-\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}}))\tilde{\xi}\\
&\ -\partial_{\mu_{t}}\Phi(B^{\gamma_{t}},\mathcal{L}_{B^{\eta_{t}}},\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}_{t}})\tilde{\xi}]d\theta\Big|^{2}\\
\leq&\ C\left([\bar{E}\|\bar{B}^{\bar{\eta}^{\lambda\bar{\xi}}}-\bar{B}^{\bar{\eta}}\|^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\|\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}^{\lambda\tilde{\xi}}}-\tilde{B}^{\tilde{\eta}}\||\tilde{\xi}|]\right)^{2}\leq C\lambda^{2}\|\xi\|_{L^{2}}^{4},\end{split}

for a constant
C
independent of
\gamma
and
\eta.
Term
A_{2}
is estimated as follows:

(214)
